Founder real-device test 2026-08-17: the video PLAYS on iPhone, and SEEK works.** Both predicted harms did not occur. **Both things are true at once and neither cancels the other:** the mechanism is real and measured, *and* no user-visible harm was observed at this file size on a real device — a browser that simply fetches the whole 2.22 MB file is entirely consistent with seeking working. ⚠️ **Evidence boundary, stated honestly: ONE 2.3 MB file, ONE device, ONE network.** That does **not** clear the gap for larger files or poor mobile networks, where fetching the whole file before playback is exactly the failure ranges exist to prevent. **CAUSE, CORRECTED:** `fastapi==0.111.0` pins Starlette ≈0.37, and **Range support landed in Starlette 0.39.0 — NOT 0.45** (0.38.6 has no range markers; 0.39.0 has `accept-ranges`, `206` and `_parse_range_header`, established by downloading the wheels and reading `responses.py` out of each). **The minimum bump is therefore `fastapi 0.115.2`, not 0.115.7** — 0.115.1 caps at `starlette<0.39.0`. **NOT FIXED — filed as `[OTHER][P2]` TECH_DEBT** with the fix path and its verification cost. ⚠️ Also observed, pre-existing and not from this deploy: **unknown paths under `/media/` return `200` + `index.html` rather than `404`** (proved with a deliberately-nonexistent control path) — an SPA fallback, which means a naive status-code check cannot prove a file is absent. **D-B4.1-1 IS DISCHARGED**; the landing returns to normal push/deploy discipline. ❌ ~~**CURRENT STATE: prod = fly 232 (hotfix) · landing in LOCAL ITERATION MODE (D-B4.1-1, founder-ratified 2026-08-12).**~~ **fly 232 = hero-spotlight horizontal-overflow hotfix**, readback-verified `revision` = **`2c1e5db772c692038fce3d6fca9dbd9f08f8d7b3`**. Defect: the page scrolled sideways with the hero text clipped left and blank space right — **founder-confirmed on a real phone**, live since B4. Cause: `.hero-spotlight` is 190% of the composer form (max-w-2xl), i.e. ~1277px centred on the viewport, so it overran the right edge on **every viewport under ~1277px** — phones, tablets and any non-maximised laptop, not just phones. Fix: `overflow-x-clip` on the first-viewport wrapper — **clip, never `hidden`** (hidden would create a scroll container and force the other axis to `auto`, and the ScrollTrigger pin is live in this build), and chosen over resizing the blob so desktop geometry is pixel-identical by construction. **Rule-7 classification + citation sweep + two shipped-doc corrections (2026-08-17). Docs-only; NOT pushed, NOT deployed; no code changed, so `pytest` stays 291 passed / 28 skipped.** **A — RULE 7 CLASSIFIED, RULE STILL NOT WRITTEN (read-only, as instructed).** 🔑 **The census found a THIRD property of `_safe_db_write` that neither the ruling nor the baton names, and it is the one that actually separates the sites: THE HELPER IS INSERT-ONLY.** It is `for rec in records: db.add(rec)` + `commit()`, so it can only ever be *called with new ORM objects*. It cannot express a bulk `query().delete()`, a bulk `query().update()`, an `on_conflict_do_update()` UPSERT, or raw `text()` SQL. **This is a capability limit, not a choice — and it is ALREADY DOCUMENTED IN-REPO**, at `api/server.py:2380-2383`, where `user_context_hash_upsert` records a Rule 7 deviation *"because the helper is INSERT-only (db.add + commit) and cannot express atomic UPSERT semantics."* **Testing the baton's three candidate principles against the real sites: "background tasks have no caller to read a return value" DOES NOT HOLD** — all five background sites swallow-and-log exactly as `_safe_db_write` would, so they are avoiding neither property; what distinguishes `_cleanup_old_records` is that it is a bulk DELETE the helper cannot express. **"Idempotency handlers must distinguish duplicate from failure" HOLDS but explains exactly ONE site** (`clerk_webhook`, and only because it was written that way deliberately — the other two webhooks make no such distinction). **"Multi-statement routines need one transaction" HOLDS for the deletion routine** (ratified exception (a)) **but is not what separates these 25** — a `_safe_db_write` call commits the whole session including dirty objects, so it would not actually have split them. **RECOMMENDED SHAPE: state the PRINCIPLE with a CHECK, never a named list** — a list of endpoints rots on the next endpoint exactly as five line-number citations have now rotted. The honest principle is roughly *"`_safe_db_write` is the required path for INSERTING new records on a request path; any site whose DML it cannot express, or which must distinguish specific exception types, states why in a comment at the call site"* — which is the form `user_context_hash_upsert` already follows unprompted. **RESIDUAL DRIFT IS REAL AND SMALL:** plain-INSERT sites in `server.py` that *could* use the helper and do not — `explain_identify_feedback` (1630), `create_feedback` (1732), `dodo_webhook` (2091). Not defects (each wraps in `try/except` and returns a proper error, arguably better than swallow-and-return-`False`), but unexplained variance. **⚠️ SCOPE GAP IS NINE SITES, NOT ONE:** Rule 7 says *"in server.py"* and therefore does not reach `usage_service.py` (7) or `cost_tracker.py` (2) at all — including the credit-deduction commits, which are the most consequence-bearing writes in the app. ❌ **CORRECTION TO MY OWN EARLIER NUMBER: there are 11 real `_safe_db_write` call sites, not 12** — my previous count included two prose mentions of the name. The in-repo comment at `:2383` also says "all 12", so it carries the same off-by-one. **B — CITATION SWEEP.** **SOP: 4 of 4 converted to symbols** — and 🔴 **THREE OF THE FOUR WERE ALREADY STALE, pointing at unrelated code**: `server.py:1448` (cited for the forward `user_id → email` lookup) now lands inside `verify_drug_interaction` computing a risk level; `server.py:1819` (cited for `cancel_subscription`) now lands inside `get_user_history`; `server.py:1605-1606` (cited for the Dodo `plan_type` flip) now lands on **blank lines** inside `explain_report`. **Deletion-feature C — PIECES #2 + #1 BUILT LOCALLY, NOT PUSHED, NOT DEPLOYED (2026-08-17).** Nothing ran against prod; the only DB touched was the **dev** branch, inside rollback-only transactions. **PIECE #2 — `_hard_delete_user()` lives at `api/services/deletion_service.py`** (a NEW module, not `server.py`: importing `api.server` runs `sentry_sdk.init`, builds the FastAPI app, adds middleware and calls `Path("static/og").mkdir()` at import time — a deletion helper must not drag that in). **It is unreachable from every route — verified, not asserted:** the only mention of it anywhere under `api/` outside its own module is a *docstring sentence in the Clerk webhook saying it is not called.* **Contract, all four points in the docstring:** does NOT commit (caller owns the transaction — required by the ratified Rule 7 exception AND by the harness, whose zero-residue guarantee IS the outer `db.rollback()`); does NOT swallow exceptions; **implements SOP Step 3 ONLY — explicitly NOT Step 1 (cancel Dodo) and NOT Step 5 (Clerk reconcile), with the misuse spelled out in the docstring in those words: a caller that invokes this and nothing else would erase the user's data while they keep getting billed**; returns per-table before/after counts for SOP Step 7. Behaviour is verbatim from the SOP at HEAD — 5 hard deletes, `api_cost_log` SET NULL (rows retained), `user_usage` FREEZE via `deleted_at` (**not** pseudonymize; FLAG #1 retired at the SOP's own L192-193), `shared_query.created_by` SET NULL (page kept). Implemented in **ORM calls, deliberately not by re-executing the SOP's SQL strings** — a helper that replayed them would make the equivalence test below a tautology. 🔴 **THE `created_by` HASH QUESTION WAS REPORTED, NOT DECIDED SILENTLY — and it forced a signature deviation the founder should rule on.** The baton specifies `_hard_delete_user(db, user_id)`; what is implemented is **`_hard_delete_user(db, user_id, *, created_by_hash)`** — required, keyword-only, no default. Reasons: `_hash_created_by()` lives at **`server.py:2818-2822`** and computing internally would mean the heavy import above; duplicating the formula would create a third copy (SOP, harness, service) to keep in sync; and an optional/defaulted parameter makes SOP **FLAG #2** — wrong-or-missing salt matches **zero** rows and silently leaves authorship links intact — the *easy* path. Required + keyword-only makes forgetting it a `TypeError` at the call site instead of a silent no-op. **Trivially reversible if the founder prefers the literal two-arg signature.** **HARNESS:** `scripts/deletion_dryrun.py` gains `--impl sql|python`, **default `sql` so today's behaviour is unchanged**. Every guard preserved byte-for-byte — verified by diffing: the `DEV_SUBSTR` allow-list, the `DELETION_DRYRUN_ALLOW` gate, `finally: db.rollback()`, and `--plan` as the no-arg default that opens no connection (the only diff line matching a guard pattern is a new help string). **BOTH PATHS RAN ON DEV AND BOTH PASSED 14/14, rolled back, zero residue** — `impl=sql` 14/14 and `impl=python` 14/14, the same asserts, which is the equivalence evidence: the SQL is the lawyer-confirmed artifact, so the helper is proven not to have diverged from the legally signed-off behaviour. The Python run also printed the Step-7 counts and they show the design-E distinction exactly: **`user_usage_total` 1→1 (RETAINED) while `user_usage_active` 1→0 (FROZEN)** — reported as two numbers on purpose, because a single `user_usage: 1→0` line in the operator log would read as "the billing row was deleted", the precise misreading the freeze exists to prevent. **PYTEST — `tests/test_deletion_coverage.py`, 6 DB-free guards** (no connection: `create_engine` is lazy). The load-bearing one asserts the model's 12 tables **partition exactly** into the 8 the routine touches + the 4 FLAG #3 declares out of scope, with both sets **derived** (`Base.metadata.tables` and the service's own `DELETION_TABLES`/`OUT_OF_SCOPE_TABLES`), never hand-typed — a hand-typed list rots the moment either side changes, which is the failure it exists to prevent. Business rule (Rule 17): **a new table holding user data is added and the deletion routine silently fails to cover it — a compliance breach against the deployed Policy §4 that nothing else would catch; the dry-run would still pass 14/14 because it only asserts on tables it already knows.** Plus the no-commit contract via a mock session, with a negative control pinning 5 deletes / 3 updates / 18 counts so "no commit" cannot be satisfied by "no work". **ALL 8 MUTATIONS CAUGHT**, including **N8 — a new user-data table appended to `sql_models.py`, the real scenario.** **PIECE #1 — `POST /api/webhooks/clerk` (`api/server.py:2181`), NON-DESTRUCTIVE.** Writes `webhook_events` **only**; **verified by inspecting the handler body with its docstring stripped — `_hard_delete_user`, `UserUsage` and `user_usage` are absent from the code and appear only in prose.** No `deleted_at`, no `.delete(`/`.update(`. **Signature reuses the Standard Webhooks scheme `dodo_webhook` already hand-implements — no `svix` package pinned, installed or needed** — and reads **both** `svix-*` and `webhook-*` header spellings, svix first. ⚠️ ~~**THE CLERK PATH IS SPEC-CORRECT BUT EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED: no real Clerk delivery has ever reached it.** Do not read "spec-correct" as "working" (Rule 18).~~ ✅ **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-17 — VERIFIED (real delivery + replay + one `webhook_events` row); the fly-234 entry above carries the measured facts. `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is now SET on prod (fly 235).** ⚠️ **The SURFACE is verified; the AUTOMATION still does not exist.** **Idempotency fixes the class instead of propagating it:** insert-first + `except IntegrityError → already_processed`, so the PK does the de-duplication and there is no check-to-write window. The two existing webhooks keep the TOCTOU defect (500 instead of `already_processed` on a concurrent duplicate) — **filed as a new `[OTHER][P2]` TECH_DEBT entry, not fixed here.** Event id prefixed **`clerk_`** (Dodo prefixes, LemonSqueezy stores a bare uuid — an unprefixed third provider is a PK-collision risk). **No migration — verified independently against `sql_models.py`, not inherited from BACKLOG:** `webhook_events.event_id` is VARCHAR **NOT NULL, `PK cols: ['event_id']`**. ~~Needs the new env var `CLERK_WEBHOOK_SECRET`.~~ ✅ **SET on prod 2026-08-17 → fly 235 (a rolling restart, so `/health` `revision` correctly stayed `2c533bf`).** Error semantics mirror the existing two: **401** bad/missing signature or stale timestamp (±300s replay window), **500** missing/malformed secret, generic messages only — **no `str(e)` anywhere (Rule 5), no `print()` (Rule 4)**. **TECH_DEBT recorded in the fly-222 split, deliberately:** *the SURFACE now exists, the AUTOMATION does not* — a Clerk-side deletion still produces **no change** to Vela's database and SOP Step 5 remains a human step. **It is NOT written as "webhook gap closed."** **RULE 7:** ratified exception (a) honoured — the deletion routine is one transaction and does not use `_safe_db_write()`. ⚠️ **A SECOND, NARROWER deviation is FLAGGED FOR RATIFICATION, not self-granted:** the Clerk handler's `except IntegrityError` cannot be expressed through `_safe_db_write()`, which catches everything and returns `False` — that would report a **database outage as `already_processed`**. ffprobe **1440×828 High yuv420p 30fps, 414 frames, 13.800s exact, 2,325,186 B (2.22 MB) = 37% of the 6 MB budget** (no CRF 23 needed). **POSTER re-taken from the ORIGINAL at t=47.00** — the cut's final frame, in the **post-scroll state** where the Summary card sits at the top of the pane, which is the better first impression now that the fix exists: 1440×828, 110,304 B. ⚠️ **ASPECT RETUNE FORCED BY THE NEW MASTER — 1440×812 → 1440×828, ratio 1.77340 → 1.73913.** Three carriers moved and each now says in-comment that the pair is **re-read from ffprobe per encode, never carried over**, because a stale pair still renders and merely **squashes the frame silently**: the `<video>` `width`/`height`, the explicit `style={{ aspectRatio }}`, and the probe's assertions. **B4.5 (local, 2026-08-14) — answer-pane auto-scroll fix (PRODUCT) + near-black panel + gradient removal + the deferred B4.4 hygiene.**
**(A) ANSWER PANE — the founder-reported defect is REAL, RESEARCH-ONLY, and was ONE line.** Investigation first: the entire repo contains **exactly one** answer-pane scroll site, `pages/research.tsx:258`, a `useEffect` on `[answer]` doing `scrollTop = scrollHeight` on **every token** — so at end-of-stream the user is parked at the BOTTOM of a long answer and must scroll UP to the Summary. **Verify and Explain do NOT share a component and do NOT duplicate it** — they are separate pages with **zero** scroll machinery (`git grep scrollTop|scrollIntoView|scrollTo` over `pages/`+`components/` returns only this site and the landing's chevron). Explain streams over SSE too but never auto-follows, so it cannot exhibit the defect; Verify does not even use `fetchEventSource`. **One fix, one surface, and nothing to carry across** — stated because Rule 19 would otherwise demand the sweep. **The completion signal is the `loading` true→false EDGE**, tracked with a `wasLoadingRef`: `loading` goes false in exactly two places, both genuine end-of-stream (`data.type === 'done'` at `research.tsx:438-439` and `onclose()` at :458) — **not a timeout, not the first gap in tokens.** ✅ **ORDERING CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED:** `loading` is *also* what swaps the raw stream for the composed render (`const sections = !loading ? parseResearchSections(...)`, plus the `ProvenanceLine`/`FallbackBanner` row, all gated on `!loading`), so both come from the SAME state change and React commits the composed DOM **before** the effect runs — the scroll lands on the finished Summary, not ahead of it. Scrolling to `0` is height-independent, so the re-render changing `scrollHeight` cannot invalidate it either. **DECISION ON FIGHTING THE USER — always snap, and the reason is that there is no user position to preserve:** the follow effect overwrites `scrollTop` on *every chunk*, so a manual scroll during generation is destroyed within one token; "the user deliberately scrolled away" is a state that **cannot survive to completion** in the current design. Given that, top-of-answer beats bottom-of-answer unconditionally. ⚠️ **Flagged, NOT changed:** making the follow YIELD to a manual scroll is a separate and larger change and was not in this baton — say so if it should be. Reduced motion gets `behavior:'auto'` (instant), everyone else `'smooth'`. **Regression test: `tests/test_research_answer_scroll.py`, 6 tests — and its scope is stated honestly in its own docstring.** They are SOURCE assertions, not behavioural: this repo has **no JS test runner**, and the completion path needs a live SSE stream, the FastAPI backend and a real credit-costing query to reach at all. What they do pin is the structure, including the one premise that can break **silently from a distance** — `test_composed_render_is_still_gated_on_loading`, because if the composed render is ever driven by a different trigger the ordering guarantee dissolves with nothing else complaining. Comments are stripped before matching (the fix is documented by a ~30-line comment naming every identifier asserted, so without stripping, deleting the whole implementation would leave the suite green). **Mutation-tested against the real file: 5/5 caught** (scroll deleted, edge guard replaced by the naive `if (loading) return`, reduced-motion ignored, stream-following removed, composed render un-gated). ⚠️ **END-TO-END NOT RUN** — a real query on `/research` is the remaining check and it is the founder's, since it needs the backend and spends credits.
**(B) NEAR-BLACK PANEL — founder-ratified, recorded as a KNOWING aesthetic decision, not a regression.** `--color-panel` **62 48 40 → 18 18 18**. This reverses B4.3's warm brown-charcoal deliberately: the founder has seen #3E3028 rendered and chose near-black anyway. What it trades away is kept in the CSS comment as the record — **the paper→panel lightness cliff returns to ~91 points (98.0% → 7.1%), WIDER than the 89 of the original ink panel**, and at **S=0%** the panel now sits **outside** the paper/hero hue family (H24°), which is the exact kinship B4.3 was built to create. **CONTRAST MEASURED IN-BROWSER from computed styles (not hand-estimated — and my hand computation agreed to within 0.01, checked precisely because the B4.1d session's estimate was wrong by 0.1):** headline/body paper@1.0 **17.95:1** (was 12.13) · sub paper@0.70 **9.08:1** (was 6.83) · CTA pill label **17.95:1** (was 12.13) · media hairline paper@0.15 **1.52:1** (decorative, not text). Every text value clears AA normal with far more margin than #3E3028 had, and with the gradient gone the panel's **weakest text is now the sub at 9.08:1**. **HAIRLINE REVERTED /20 → /15**: the B4.3 raise was compensation for the panel *lightening*, and that cause is gone — near-black is **darker than the ink the /15 hairline originally sat on** (1.52:1 now vs 1.55:1 historically = the original look restored; /20 would read 1.82:1). One-token flip if the founder disagrees.
**(C) GRADIENT REMOVED, headline solid + BOLD.** `.panel-gradient-text` is gone from the component (`spanClassName` dropped, `h2` gains `font-bold`; no color class on purpose — the panel wrapper's `text-paper` is inherited at full strength) and **entirely from `globals.css`**: the base rule, the per-line `background-size: 100% 1lh` tile, the `::selection` rule and the `forced-colors` fallback, replaced by a tombstone explaining that **all three existed only to make clipped text safe** and guard nothing once the glyphs have real ink. **The two B4.1d pytest guards are DELETED with their helpers** (`_GLOBALS`, `_CSS_COMMENT`, `_css`, `_gradient_block`) — not left in place, because `_gradient_block()` did `css.index()` on comment-stripped CSS and would have raised **ValueError**: a test that can only error is dead weight, not safety. **VERIFIED AT COMPUTED-STYLE LEVEL, which is stronger than any class check:** span `color` = paper, **`-webkit-text-fill-color` = paper (not transparent)**, **`background-image` = none**, class = `inline-block`. **The word reveal survives**: 24 spans, and forward/reverse readings are identical (below).
**(D) DEFERRED HYGIENE — all four done.** **D1** masters moved to **`assets-source/media/`** with a README carrying the exact re-derivation commands and the reason segment 1 ends at 31.00; `out/` drops **19.9 MB → 6.66 MB** and `out/media` now holds **exactly the two shipped files**. The PNG moved via `git mv` (history preserved); the 11.78 MB master was **untracked** and is now tracked for the first time. **D2** `controls` at `<md`: **verified at 375px, hydrated — `controls: true`, `paused: true`, `preload="none"`, `networkState: 1 (IDLE)`, mp4 NOT fetched** (controls do not fetch media, so a phone still pays only the 95 KB poster until it taps), while **desktop stays `controls: false`** and observer-driven. **D3** the stale `md:min-h-[80vh]` header comment is corrected to `md:h-[92vh]` with a note that it was stale since B4.3 R2. **D4** Sentry filed as `[OTHER][P2]` with filesystem evidence — ⚠️ **and the entry CORRECTS the framing it was handed**: `next.config.ts:5` sets `output: 'export'`, so **there is no Next.js server in production** and the "SDK is not initialized on the server" warning is moot; real server-side reporting is the **FastAPI backend's own independent `sentry_sdk.init()`** (`api/server.py:17-31`, different SDK, different env var `SENTRY_DSN`). **The real exposure is the CLIENT**, still on the deprecated `sentry.client.config.ts` instead of `instrumentation-client.ts` — and browser errors are the *only* Next-side errors a static export has. Nav-table counts repaired 65→66 / 122→123.
**VERIFIED:** `tsc` 0 · **pytest 283 passed / 28 skipped** (279 → +6 new scroll guards −2 deleted gradient guards) · build clean · probe **52 → 62 checks, 62/62** · **ALL 7 new B4.5 probe mutations caught** (gradient class reattached, headline un-bolded, hairline reverted, token reverted, controls SET removed, controls CLEAR removed, a master shipped into `out/media`) **and ALL 5 research-scroll mutations caught**. **LIVE (hidden-window guard fired FIRST):** panel **767px = 92.0vh constant** at p=0.30/0.60/0.90 and at widths 1024/1050/1280 · media **52.6%** of panel · video ratio **1.7734** · **375px hOverflow 0** · reveal **0/24 at p=0.301 → 8/24 at p=0.600 → 24/24 at p=0.900**, `max-width` matching the formula EXACTLY at every position (1101 / 1178 / 1254px), and **REV p=0.60 IDENTICAL to FWD p=0.60** (1178px, 8/24) — the reveal is still a pure function of scroll position after the gradient removal. ⚠️ **INSTRUMENT LIMIT + TWO SELF-CAUGHT ERRORS:** rAF measured **0 frames in 500 ms** while the window was occluded, so a naive sweep read `revealed: 0` everywhere and would have reported the reveal BROKEN — the whole sweep was re-taken with the B4.1c screenshot-flush interleave, and every reading above carries a hydration/frame control. **(i)** I wrote a probe check grepping the built CSS for `background-clip: text` — **unsound in BOTH directions**: Tailwind legitimately emits `.bg-clip-text` because `Navbar`/`PlanBadge`/`pages/index.tsx:498` use it, *and* the source matched on **this probe's own tombstone comment**. Replaced with an assertion that the headline spans carry only the layout class. **(ii)** The landing token guard **caught my own comment** — I put the near-black hex literal in a `LandingSections.tsx` comment, which that file forbids including in prose. Reworded rather than weakening the guard.

**B4.3-R4 (local, 2026-08-14) — the demo VIDEO lands; R4 is no longer blocked.** ffmpeg 9.0 IS installed but NOT on PATH (winget's Links shim did not take); absolute paths used throughout, both binaries verified before starting. **ffprobe CONFIRMS the B4.3 box-parse exactly** — 1662×938 h264 30fps, 39.333s, **aac 192 kbps present**, 12,353,959 B — so that finding was right and is now instrumented rather than ad-hoc. **PHASE 0 TIMELINE** (14 frames at 3s + 21 targeted frames + 48 scene changes at thr 0.04): 9.567 SEARCH CLICKED · 12–21 three loading states · **~21.6 first tokens stream in** · **27.567 REFERENCES POPULATE** (`REFERENCES (5)`, DailyMed 3 / PubMed 2, `View source`) · ~31.1 the page scrolls and `Query time: 16.06s` enters · **37.233 FINAL COMPOSED RENDER** (Summary + Clinical Notes cards) then **STATIC for the last 2.100s**. 🔴 **CORRECTION APPLIED TO THE APPROVED CUT: segment 1's approved out of 31.40 STILL SHOWED `Query time: 16.06s`** — the line enters between **31.00 and 31.20**, earlier than my Phase-0 "~31.6" estimate, which is the number the founder's boundary was derived from. Cut at **31.00** so Option A′ actually does what it was chosen to do (exclude the performance claim); the founder was told, not silently overridden. Final cut **21.30→31.00 + 35.10→38.60 = 13.200s**. ⚠️ **THE SEAM IS A VISIBLE JUMP, NOT CONTINUOUS SCROLLING** — measured PSNR across the join **14.83 dB at 31.40→35.10, 15.63 dB at 31.00→35.10** (identical content, but the page scroll offset differs and the nav bar pops in). Reported, not silently swapped; the founder's accepted fallback (plain Option A, 21.30→38.60, 17.3s, query-time visible ~3.3s) is **one re-encode away and needs ZERO Phase-2 change** — the wiring is invariant to which cut wins. **ENCODE:** ONE pass from the ORIGINAL via `filter_complex` `trim`+`concat` (no intermediate files, never an encode of an encode); `-pix_fmt yuv420p` added for browser compat, which the baton did not specify. **OUTPUT VERIFIED TWO WAYS** — box parse `ftyp → moov → free → mdat` = **faststart YES**, handlers `vide, mdir` only = **audio tracks 0**; ffprobe **1440×812 High yuv420p 30 fps, 396 frames, 13.200s, 2,013,596 B (1.92 MB, 1.22 Mbps)** = **32% of the 6 MB budget, so CRF 23 was not needed**. The box parser is committed as **`tests/probes/b43_mp4_boxes.mjs`** + result JSON (**Rule 20** — B4.3's parse left no artifact and was unverifiable by anyone else); **NEGATIVE-CONTROLLED**: run against the original it reports faststart NO + 1 audio track and exits 1, so it can fail. **Poster** taken from the **ORIGINAL at t=38.00** (= 12.60s into the cut), **not from the encode**, to avoid compounding h264 artifacts into a JPEG — 1440×812, 96,918 B (94.6 KB). The B4.2 PNG is now **unreferenced by product code** (only STATE + the probe name it). All four media files are **TRACKED** (`git check-ignore` returns nothing). ⚠️ **`out/` now ships 13.2 MB of DEAD media** — `public/` is copied wholesale, so the 11.78 MB original and the 859 KB PNG land in the export with nothing referencing them; the baton said delete nothing, so this is a founder call. **WIRING:** `<video muted loop playsInline preload="none" poster>` replaces `next/image` (import removed). 🔴 **SPEC MISMATCH FLAGGED, NOT GUESSED: the `aspect-[1975/1114]` literal the baton asked me to retune DOES NOT EXIST** — B4.3 R2 removed it and `b41d_panel_smoke.mjs:110` **actively asserts its absence**; the intrinsic ratio lived in next/image's `width`/`height`. Carried across as **`width=1440 height=812` + an explicit `aspect-ratio:1440 / 812`** so the zero-CLS guarantee does not depend on UA behaviour for `<video>`. **DEVIATION (stated): NO `autoplay` attribute.** The attribute starts playback as soon as the element parses, which would leave "never plays off-screen" resting on a browser heuristic (Chrome defers off-screen muted autoplay; Firefox does not); playback is IntersectionObserver-driven only, and the probe pins the attribute's ABSENCE. **VERIFIED LIVE (hidden-window guard fired FIRST — `visibilityState: hidden`):** panel **767px = 92.0vh at 1024 / 1050 / 1280px width — height CONSTANT**, media **52.6% of panel** (above the 40% floor), video ratio **1.7734 = exactly 1440/812, no squash**, hOverflow 0. Observer contract verified **both ways**: in viewport → `paused:false` **and the mp4 fetch only starts then** (`preload="none"` holds — mp4 NOT fetched at rest); scrolled away (video 1147px below the fold) → `paused:true`. **375px: hOverflow 0** (the fly-232 class is not reintroduced), video width-driven 276px = panel inner width, **paused, no controls, mp4 NEVER fetched** — mobile pays only the 95 KB poster. **Reduced motion: video in viewport yet `paused:true`, `controls:true`, mp4 never fetched, scroll handler off.** ⚠️ **INSTRUMENT LIMITS, ALL SELF-CAUGHT:** (a) rAF measured **0 frames in 3000 ms** while the window was occluded, so the p=1 width expand could not be driven by the handler — panel geometry was measured by writing the handler's own formula value (1280 = min(80rem, 94vw)) directly, which tests the GEOMETRY claim, not the handler; the handler itself was observed writing 1050px at p=0.1. **B4.3 (local, 2026-08-14) — R1/R2/R3 + P0.3 shipped; ~~R4 (video) BLOCKED~~ → R4 LANDED at B4.3-R4 above.** 🔴 **The video could not be prepared: ffmpeg/ffprobe is not installed anywhere on this machine**, and `Research_Demo_Video.mp4` needs THREE fixes before it can ship — I probed the container by parsing the ISO-BMFF boxes directly (no ffprobe needed) and it is **1662×938 avc1, 39.32s, 12,353,959 bytes (11.78 MB), 2.51 Mbps**, top-level order `ftyp → uuid → mdat → moov`, i.e. **NOT faststart** (moov is last → progressive playback stalls), **HAS an mp4a audio track** (must be stripped), and is **~2× the ~6MB budget**. 5. **`unextractable_summary()` is session-cumulative and NON-CLEARING, and a JSON reader cannot see that** — in a two-dump run BOTH dumps carry the whole session's set, not their own section's. ⚠️ **Same defect shape this car closed one level up**: the caveat lives where the author reads it (`scripts/_pmid_guard.py:63-64`), not where the reader does. → `TECH_DEBT.md` → the `[DONE]` entry headed *"THE source_id DIGIT-SCRAPE CLASS IS CLOSED BY A RULE, NOT BY A LIST"*.

### Task-A Answer-Quality QA + B2C/B2B decision (2026-06-15)

**Task-A medical-answer-quality QA is COMPLETE** (measurement only — NO product change, NO route decision made). Three layers run this session; all artifacts in `tests/results/` (gitignored):

- **Stage 1 — full 127-case golden eval** (`golden_results_20260613_144748.json`): raw 74.8% but heavily contaminated — 22/24 FAILs were harness/guard artifacts (v179 UI-language harness staleness ×6; guard over-block of colloquial symptom queries ×11; non-Latin-script identify ×4; transient ×1). Guard 100%, PHI 100%, Explain `no_fabricated_citations` hard-floor 0 violations, Explain disclaimer 17/17. **2 REAL content gaps**: R10 (digoxin toxicity omits nausea/vomiting + visual disturbances) + the persistent canonical-term under-coverage (R03 CHA₂DS₂-VASc, R16/R20 safer alternatives); R09 improved to PASS.
- **Stage 2 — citation-truth, 18 usable answers** (`citation_truth_20260614_113716.json`): PMID existence 80/80 (100%, **no fabricated PMIDs**); `has_hallucination` 0/20; mean source_support 90.6. Claim-support over 180 pairs: 45.6% supported / 50% partial / 3.9% unsupported / **0.6% contradicts (1)** / 0% wrong_scope. The 1 CONTRADICTS = polypharmacy/mortality reversal (answer "increased death risk" vs abstract OR 0.78 LOWER, PMID 35268461). B07 = confident AF-stroke-prevention answer with **0 citations**. **Lower-bound** (abstract-only judging over-counts unsupported).
- **Stage 3 — adversarial contradiction, 19 queries** (`adversarial_contradiction_20260614_232655.json`): famous-set reversal 3/16 = **18.8%** (C01 polypharmacy, C03 intensive-glucose, C15 DBP J-curve); cohort-specific 0/3 (underpowered, NOT evidence of safety); 4/186 pairs. C01 reproduced 3× ("reverts counterintuitive finding to intuition"). **Upper-bound** (loaded toward failure). So the true rate sits between ~0.6% (random) and ~19% (adversarial).

**Medical-advisor review:** REJECTED direct B2C public launch in the current state — the "reverses a counterintuitive finding *and* cites a real source" failure is clinically + legally unacceptable (TW 醫師法 §28 密醫罪, FDA device exposure). Recommended **3-phase roadmap**: **Phase 1 (≤1mo)** defensive pivot to B2B (pause B2C, reposition as clinician/pharmacist/researcher literature tool, HCP credential verification at signup, algorithm-oversight committee) → FDA-2026 non-device CDS exemption + avoids 密醫罪; **Phase 2 (3–6mo)** contradiction circuit-breaker + HealthBench-DDI ≥99% + HIPAA-2026 / ONC HTI-1; **Phase 3** SaMD registration (TFDA Class 2 + FDA 510(k)/De Novo) before gradual B2C.

**Prompt-fix attempt + re-measurement (2026-06-15, commit `96806b5`):** the direction-of-effect fidelity instruction (a 3-bullet block in `generator.py`) was applied and re-measured across 7 adversarial runs (2 baseline + 3 post-fix + the prior external baseline + a B01 Stage-2 recheck). **Verdict: PARTIAL mitigation — do NOT read it as "the rate dropped."** Post-fix adversarial famous-set reversal was **19% / 25% / 19%**, INSIDE the baseline band **19% / 25% / 38%** (overlapping; variance-dominated). What genuinely improved: the two stable, reproducible **NEUTRAL-FRAMED** reversers **C01** (polypharmacy/AF) and **C13** (smoker's-paradox STEMI) resolved to faithful (`same`) in all 3 post-fix runs — C01 stayed faithful even when retrieval cited a different PMID (35155632 vs 35268461), so the effect-statistic anchor is a general mechanism, not memorization (live probe showed "lower odds, OR 0.78"). **KEY NEW FINDING — FRAMING-SENSITIVITY:** on the loaded-premise Stage-2 query B01 ("*Why is polypharmacy a serious problem…*"), PMID 35268461 **STILL CONTRADICTS** under the fix — the premise overrides the instruction. Neutral framing fixed; loaded framing NOT — and real B2C users ask loaded questions. Also: C15 (conflicting-evidence + unstable retrieval) did not move; a mild `not_directional` drift (~5.3→~8.7) on non-targeted famous cases to watch; holdout held 0/5 in all 3 runs (no over-correction — but "no new harm" ≠ generalization to unseen reversal modes).

**Model-swap attempt + audit (2026-06-15, o4-mini via the §2.1 Provider abstraction; DONE — NEGATIVE):** swapping the Research generation model gpt-4.1 → o4-mini (reasoning model; needed the provider param-contract fix `455bcb5` to run) did **NOT** fix loaded-framing reversal. The initial o4-mini B01 read was counts-only ("3/3 no-contradict") and **did NOT reproduce**; a **persisted, full-prose** re-run (`tests/results/recheck_20260615_225242.json`, judge gpt-4.1) gave **0/3 fidelity**: run2 + run3 **CONTRADICTS** PMID 35268461 (asserting higher all-cause death "OR 1.82" while citing the OR-0.78-*lower* source — conflated the multimorbidity ORs onto polypharmacy); run1's "no-contradict" was an **evasion** (routed risk through multimorbidity, judge PARTIAL), not a faithful statement of OR 0.78. **No run stated the OR-0.78 lower-death finding.** **Framing-sensitivity is model-swap-resistant** — the SAME o4-mini, SAME PMID, on the NEUTRAL query (C01) stated *"counterintuitively linked to lower odds of all-cause death (OR 0.78)"* faithfully (even flagging the paradox) and attributed other cohorts' higher-mortality HRs to their sources; on the LOADED query (B01) it reversed 2/3. **Only framing differs.** **Scope caveat:** ONE loaded query (B01), 2/3 — the same small-sample limit as the prior optimistic read; not generalized. A GPT-5.4 comparison is an optional last check with LOW expected value (the failure looks systemic, not compute-limited).

**STRUCTURAL FINDING — the reversal problem is TWO distinct failure modes (2026-06-16; Phase-3 eval `evalrun_{o4-mini,gpt-4.1}_20260616` + N=10 retrieval-recall diagnostic `retrieval_recall_20260616_133331.json`, committed `4248dfd`):** this overturns the implicit "bad answers all come from generation" assumption. **(1) GENERATION-reversal (polypharmacy 35268461):** the protective OR-0.78 source is **reliably retrieved at loaded framing** (10/10 in pool AND final at L1/L2, rank 2, never reranked out) — yet the model still states the reversed direction. So the B01 reversals happened **with the source in hand** → a generation failure. A post-hoc direction (NLI) checker is the matching defense. **(2) RETRIEVAL-recall failure (obesity 37290898):** the protective paradox source is **often never retrieved** — loaded OB-L2 = **0/10 in pool** (recall-miss: the loaded rewrite → harm-mechanism/HFpEF papers, paradox doc never enters); neutral OB-L0 = in pool 7/10 but **reranked OUT 7/10** (enters low-scored ~rank 14, reranker keeps 5 newer higher-scored papers). The model **literally never sees** the paradox source and answers from harm-side papers → **a direction-checker CANNOT catch this** (no source in the answer's citations to compare against). Needs retrieval-side fixes. **Sub-findings:** neither anchor is in the local 690-doc store (100% FDA labels) — both are live-PubMed + LLM-rewrite, NOT local indexing. Framing biases retrieval itself, **oppositely by topic**: polypharmacy loaded recalls MORE (L2 10/10 vs L0 5/10), obesity loaded SUPPRESSES (L2 0/10 vs L0 7/10) — for obesity, framing and retrieval are the SAME root cause. **Hypothesis (unverified):** anchor title wording (polypharmacy paper = "Multimorbidity and Polypharmacy… Clinical Outcomes" matches loaded harm terms; obesity paper = neutral "Body mass index and survival" → loaded rewrite drifts off it). o4-mini vs gpt-4.1 on clean anchor-retrieved cases tied 3/6 each (same 3) — model-swap still doesn't fix generation-reversal. **Caveat:** single query-wording per topic, N=10, 2 topics — strong directional signal, not generalized; title-driver is a hypothesis.

**(i) RETRIEVAL prompt-fix attempt — FAILED + reverted (2026-06-16; `retrieval_recall_20260616_141620.json` vs `_133331.json`; retriever.py back at HEAD, NO commit):** the first retrieval-stabilization lever — a prompt-level **valence-neutral query-rewrite** instruction (strip the loaded premise from `_rewrite_query`) — failed both gates. **Step A (target):** OB-L2 anchor recall **0/10 → 0/10, no movement** — root cause is NOT valence but a **TERMINOLOGY mismatch**: the rewrite emits the lay term "obesity" while the anchor 37290898's title is "*Body mass index and survival*", so PubMed never matches it (neutralizing the premise can't fix obesity↔BMI). **Step B (regression guard) TRIPPED HARD:** **P-L1 collapsed 10/10 → 0/10** (deterministic, prompt-caused not variance — the neutralized rewrite shifted terms enough to lose a previously-reliable anchor), OB-L0 pool 7/10 → 0/10 — while P-L0 *improved* 5→10 in the SAME edit. **KEY LESSON:** a prompt-level "be neutral" instruction is **too BLUNT a lever** — anchor recall is highly sensitive to the precise search-term string, so a global rewrite instruction perturbs every query's terms unpredictably (fixes one, breaks another); there is **no single global rewrite instruction that simultaneously optimizes recall across topics** (P-L0 up while P-L1 down under the same edit proves it). **Caveat:** single query-wording per topic, N=10, 2 topics — directional, not a generalized "retrieval is unfixable" claim; the obesity↔BMI terminology cause is established for this anchor, the broader pattern is a hypothesis.

**③ DIRECTION-CHECKER prototype (the generation-side defense, BACKLOG §706a (b)) — 3 rounds (2026-06-16; cheap gpt-4.1-mini + factor-focused prompt; persisted answers `recheck_20260615_225242.json` + adversarial JSONs):** **the CORE (direction comparison) is validated; the WRAPPER (decomposition + aggregation) is where the risk lives.** **Round 1+2 (synthetic, single sentences):** detection 4/4 then 8/8 on known reversals, **0 false-positive on clean faithful**, conflation-trap 4/4 cross-topic (it correctly compares the claim's factor to *that factor's* direction even when the abstract reports both directions), terse abstracts 6/6 — so the cheap checker's direction logic generalizes. **Round 3 (REAL messy multi-claim answers, decompose→anchor→aggregate, polypharmacy):** detection **3/4 (one FALSE NEGATIVE)**, FP **1/2** — and every failure is in the WRAPPER, not the direction logic: **(1) decomposition-anchoring FN [the gating risk]** — B01-run2's real reversal slipped through because the decomposer extracted the claim with **empty cited_pmids** (nothing to compare); the *identical* claim in B01-run3 was caught because the decomposer happened to propagate the PMID — same reversal, opposite outcome, **false safety**; **(2) aggregation over-flag FP** — C01-115632 (faithful, correctly said OR 0.78 lower) flagged on a *different* sub-claim ("polypharmacy increases composite thrombotic events") that contradicts one cited source (32509316 null on stroke/SE) while agreeing with another (36495662) → the "ANY claim contradicts ANY source → flag" rule is too aggressive; **(3) over-splitting** (13–21 atomic claims/answer). **NEXT to evaluate — Path b-coarse:** a **WHOLE-ANSWER vs ANCHOR** direction comparison (compare the answer's overall directional stance on the key factor against the protective anchor's effect statistic directly, **bypassing per-claim decomposition/anchoring**) — hypothesis: catches the B01-run2 reversal the per-claim version missed. **Caveats:** one topic (polypharmacy), 7 real answers; the FP + VAGUE flag are debatable sub-claim tensions (so 1/2 FP is a ceiling — the protective-reversal itself was never falsely flagged); the tooling needed a mid-run fix (first attempt VOID — decomposition truncated at max_tokens=600). **Round 4 (Path b-coarse — whole-answer vs anchor, no decomposition):** fixes the gating FN — caught B01-run2 (the reversal per-claim missed) and all 4 real reversals (**detection 4/4**); its 1 FP is a genuinely-mixed answer (leads "higher HR 1.36-1.84", footnotes OR 0.78) so the flag is defensible; trade-off = coarse only sees the main-factor-vs-one-anchor reversal (blind to secondary-factor reversals) and needs an anchor-selection step. **Round 5 (anchor-selection validation — the last gating risk):** **naive selection ("the source the answer's main claim is based on") is CIRCULAR + whitewashing** — 0/3 on the confusion cases (answers citing BOTH protective 35268461-lower AND a higher source), it picks the higher source agreeing with the answer's reversed claim → whitewashes the reversal into a FALSE NEGATIVE (all wrong picks opposite-direction; this would gut the 4/4). The circularity DEFAULTS TO WHITEWASH (auto-finds the source that makes a reversed answer look fine). **ESCAPE (non-circular):** select by **COUNTERINTUITIVENESS-vs-clinical-prior** (which cited source reports the most surprising finding vs common medical assumption, judged by the source's OWN finding + the model's prior, INDEPENDENT of the answer's claim) — recovered **3/3** on the confusion cases; the prior is used only for selection, the direction judgment still comes from answer-vs-source → no circularity. **FOOTGUN:** requires **FULL abstracts** — with 700-char truncation it was 0/3 (the OR-0.78 sentence is buried deep in a stat-dense abstract that leads with the higher finding); full abstracts → 3/3. **VERDICT: coarse ③ is VIABLE but only under (counterintuitiveness-selection + full-abstract), NEVER naive selection.** **Open deps to pin before production:** (a) cross-topic prior reliability; (b) answers citing multiple competing counterintuitive sources (untested). **(a) DONE — VALIDATED 2026-06-17 (`anchor_prior_crosstab_20260617.json`, 6 topics across crisp/paradox/fuzzy bins, cheap gpt-4.1-mini):** counterintuitiveness-selection picked the correct anchor **5/5 on all confusion topics** (incl. 2 crisp paradoxes — obesity, ACCORD inverted-direction — and 2 fuzzy — HRT, alcohol); **0 mis-picks.** The bare prior was correct 5/6 (the one miss = HRT, a debatable GT-labeling artifact) — but **selection was MORE robust than the prior** (it reads the source's explicit effect size, so the one prior-miss still selected right). **A confidence gate is NOT the answer:** zero selection failures to gate, and confidence wouldn't discriminate anyway (the shaky HRT prior was high-confidence 0.8; the most-contested topic alcohol correctly drew the lowest conf 0.7). **Verdict: reliable as-is in this test; residual risk = a prior-miss that propagates to a selection error (not observed) — partly stressed by dep (b).** Caveats: 6 topics, synthetic answers (only polypharmacy real), one model.

**GENERATION-stage question-neutralization probe (2026-06-17, `neutralization_generation_20260617_105516.json`; polypharmacy/35268461 ONLY — proven retrieved, so retrieval is NOT a variable; RETRIEVE-ONCE-FREEZE-CONTEXT, generation-stage only, retrieval query untouched — NOT a repeat of the failed (i)):** decides the ③ division of labor. Retrieved once with the loaded B01 query, **35268461 confirmed in the frozen context**, then generated 4 phrasings × N=5 against the IDENTICAL frozen docs (current gpt-4.1 generator incl. fix `96806b5`), gpt-4.1 fidelity judge. **Result: loaded→neutral reversal 3/10 → 0/10 — neutralizing only the generator's question ELIMINATES the reversal** (neutral arm 10/10 faithful, both neutral phrasings agree → robust across 2 neutral phrasings on polypharmacy (single topic; cross-topic neutralization untested)). **In-run loaded control reproduced reversal (3/10)** → the fix did NOT fully solve it (control valid). **KEY NUANCE — loaded reversal is WORDING-SPECIFIC:** the two loaded phrasings DISAGREE — L1 (B01's vague "serious problem in elderly Asian patients") reverses 3/5, but L2 ("why does polypharmacy *worsen survival*") is **faithful 5/5** (the more-explicit premise self-corrected with OR 0.78). So "loaded→reversal" is not uniform — only the vague-harm-premise wording triggers it. **VERDICT (bin 1 — reversal ~eliminated under neutral): framing (the loaded premise) is the MAIN cause of the reversals that occur → front-end question-neutralization = a cheap effective pre-filter, ③ = a thin backstop; dep (b) pass-threshold = MEDIUM (the neutralization relaxation applies to the framing-induced-reversal path ONLY; dep (b) measures the source-multiplicity path, which front-end neutralization does NOT address — so the relaxation does NOT transfer). dep (b) subsequently PASSED at medium (4/4 correct-pick, see below).** Caveats: single topic, N=5/variant, one generator, 1 frozen context, synthetic neutralization phrasings; conditional on the anchor being retrieved (proven polypharmacy, unknown elsewhere); directional division-of-labor signal, not a production metric.

**dep (b) — competing counterintuitive sources, MEDIUM threshold — DONE/PASS (2026-06-17, `dep_b_competing_sources_20260617.json`):** the last untested selection stressor — when an answer cites TWO opposite-direction counterintuitive sources, does counterintuitiveness-selection still pick the right anchor (the source-multiplicity path, which front-end neutralization does NOT address). 4 two-source bundles (1 real polypharmacy reversal + 3 cross-topic synthetic, full abstracts): **(a) correct-pick 4/4, (b) whitewash 0, (c) fail-safe 0 → MEDIUM PASS** (the bar = pick right OR fail safe; achieved by picking right every time). It handled the two hardest: **glucose** (counterintuitive anchor is the *higher*-mortality ACCORD, INVERTED vs the others — still correct) and **HRT** (genuinely fuzzy-prior competing) — it reads each source's explicit effect size + prior, so a competing opposite-direction source did NOT confuse it. **Residual (carry):** the **fail-safe (abstain/low-conf) leg was NEVER exercised** (selection was accurate) → unproven; confidence was uniformly 0.9 even on contested HRT (the existing calibration debt persists → the fail-safe backstop is unreliable IF a future competing case stumps the selector). Caveats: synthetic answers except polypharmacy, 4 bundles, one model, single answer/bundle. **With dep (b) passing, selection ACCURACY is validated (dep a 5/5 + dep b 4/4). BUT the MEDIUM-threshold fail-safe leg (selector abstains/low-confidence when it cannot cleanly pick) was NEVER exercised — selection was accurate every time — and dep (a)+(b) both show the selector is OVERCONFIDENT on contested topics (uniform 0.9, wrong-but-0.8 on HRT). So the fail-safe safety net is UNVALIDATED and likely UNRELIABLE. DESIGN CONSTRAINT for production ③ (carry into the build spec): the flag/withhold trigger must NOT depend on the selector's self-reported confidence (it won't abstain — it will mis-pick confidently = the dangerous whitewash mode). Instead, trigger flag STRUCTURALLY — detect the *situation* 'answer cites ≥2 opposite-direction counterintuitive sources on the same factor→outcome' and flag unconditionally, regardless of selector confidence. Safety rests on situation-detection, not on the selector grading itself. What's left is PRODUCTION BUILD (engineering): the front-end vague-loaded-neutralization mechanism (unbuilt, §706a (b2) gap) + coarse ③ integration (unbuilt).**

**C1 (③) SHADOW BUILD — BUILT + VALIDATED → FAILED real-data shadow validation (2026-06-17, `direction_shadow_20260617_143022.json` + `…_rescore_nostruct_151126.json`; plan-back `C1_inrepo_planback.md`; code committed locally, NOT pushed, Fly deploy DEFERRED):** first prod-code touch in this chain. Gate 1 (credit probe through the real provider path) HEALTHY; Gate 2 push = no-op (`96806b5`/`fdd624c` already on origin/main); Fly deploy deferred (founder) — baseline captured in-process on HEAD (gpt-4.1, `96806b5` live). Built: `api/services/direction_checker.py` (async port of the validated core, 4 invariants), `server.py` shadow hook `_run_direction_check_background` behind `DIRECTION_CHECK_SHADOW` (default OFF, mirrors `_run_judge_background`, touches no SSE/answer), `scripts/direction_shadow_eval.py` (+ `_rescore`). Corpus frozen pre-generation (`tests/direction_shadow_corpus.json`: full 127 golden + 24 adversarial; live subset 43 = 25 targeted + 18 clean-faithful, clean criterion frozen first). **REAL NUMBERS — C1 as-built is NOT deployable (do NOT enable the flag): structural-ON detection 11/11 but FP 12/16 (75%), flags 37/43 (86% of ALL answers); structural-OFF (primary whole-answer-vs-anchor only) detection 0/11, FP 1/16.** The "11/11" is an ARTIFACT of the structural trigger rubber-stamping `two_opposite_counterintuitive=True` on real 4–5-source retrievals (35/43 verdict=structural, incl. unambiguous single-direction topics — peanut-LEAP, beta-blockers-HFrEF, digoxin, serotonin-syndrome) → no precision. The primary path detects NOTHING because **C1's own cheap anchor-scorer fell into the exact B01 conflation trap it exists to catch** (P-L1: read the multimorbidity OR 2.04 instead of polypharmacy OR 0.78 from the multi-stat abstract) + the deterministic `_opposite` can't handle "mixed" anchors (OB-L1). **VERDICT: the dep(a) 5/5 / dep(b) 4/4 / coarse 4/4 feasibility validations used hand-curated synthetic bundles + curated answers and did NOT generalize to the real pipeline — on real retrievals C1 is unusable in BOTH configs (structural = no precision; primary = no recall). C1 needs FUNDAMENTAL REWORK, not tuning.** Shadow-first caught it before any user exposure (the build's purpose). Caveats: 43 cases, 1 generator, 1 run; FP denom (16) is direction-ADJACENT (C1's mis-fire rate on the answers it acts on), NOT a site-wide rate. → TECH_DEBT (new C1 entry) + BACKLOG §706a (b)/(c).

**C1 follow-up DIAGNOSTIC — conflation is a CHEAP-MODEL artifact, NOT task-intrinsic (2026-06-17, `scripts/direction_extract_probe.py`, `direction_extract_probe_20260617_153856.json`; route now B2B-interim so advisory, not launch-gating; $0.022, 5 cases, no regeneration):** isolated re-run of ONLY the two ③ sub-tasks with a STRONGER model (gpt-4.1, not mini) on captured answers. **(a) extraction:** gpt-4.1 cleanly disentangled the multi-stat conflation trap — P-L1 + P-L2 both got polypharmacy `OR 0.78 (decreases)` AND named the `multimorbidity OR 2.04 (increases)` trap (2/2 — the exact case the mini scorer failed); clean controls 2/2. **(b) LLM comparator** (replacing deterministic `_opposite`): detection 2/2 on conflation reversals, FP 0/2 on clean. **VERDICT: C1's primary-path failure = cheap scorer + brittle comparator, NOT the task → ③ HAS a path as a thin B2B backstop IF built with a strong scorer (gpt-4.1, cents/answer) + LLM comparator.** STILL UNRESOLVED (don't over-read 2/2): the structural over-fire (75% FP) is model-independent and must be DROPPED; anchor-SELECTION over real retrievals untested (probe fed the known anchor); single-source "mixed"/U-shaped abstracts still oversimplified even by gpt-4.1 (OB-L1 dropped the U-shaped caveat). So ③ is not dead, but "viable" is CONDITIONAL on drop-structural + prove-selection-at-scale + handle-mixed.

**RETRIEVAL-RECALL REFUSAL lever — SHADOW DETECTION BUILT + VALIDATED → PASSES (2026-06-18, `retrieval_refusal_20260618_095609.json` + `_prior_probe_095754.json`; plan-back `retrieval_refusal_planback.md`; code committed locally, NOT pushed, Fly deploy deferred, flag default OFF):** covers the failure ③/C1 is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to — a confident answer where the protective/counter source was NEVER retrieved (the obesity-type retrieval-miss; the worst-case that is LIVE now for walk-in users). Route is B2B-interim; this is the advisor's "if the pool is one-directional, refuse to advise without counter-evidence" circuit-breaker + future SaMD defense arch. Gate 1 HEALTHY. **Design = H1 (POOL-STANCE-SPREAD × CONTESTED-PRIOR): refuse = (pool one-sided on factor→outcome) AND (a counter-direction is genuinely contested in CURRENT literature).** Built: `api/services/retrieval_refusal.py` (async, gpt-4.1 — mini conflates), `server.py` shadow hook `_run_retrieval_refusal_background` behind `RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` (default OFF, mirrors the C1 hook, touches no SSE/answer), `scripts/retrieval_refusal_eval.py` + `_prior_probe.py`. Corpus frozen pre-run (`tests/retrieval_refusal_corpus.json`: detection OB-L0/1/2 + complete-pool P-L1/2 + over-refusal control n=9 incl. counterintuitive-but-SETTLED stress cases; criterion frozen first). Reused captured pools (no re-retrieval). Reads the FINAL pool the generator saw (`documents`). Mid-build refinement: the per-source read is STANCE/valence (harmful/beneficial/mixed), not strict mortality-HR — a harm-framed mechanism review counts as harmful (spot-checked: OB-L2 run2's `beneficial` reads are REAL paradox mentions). **REAL NUMBERS — the OPPOSITE of C1: DETECTION 3/3 retrieval-miss cases fired (by run 7/9; the 2 non-fires correctly had paradox-conveying pools); OVER-REFUSAL 0/9 (with 8/9 one-sided pools = the prior leg genuinely stressed); complete-pool 0/2 over-fire; PRIOR generalization 10/10 contested-vs-settled across diverse topics (glucose-ACCORD/DBP/alcohol/sodium/HRT/obesity True; peanut/BB-HFrEF/smoking/statins False); cost $0.16.** The C1 "flag everything" failure did NOT recur — the prior leg separates genuinely-contested (obesity paradox → refuse) from counterintuitive-but-settled (CAST/LEAP/smoking-PD → don't refuse). H2/H3 NOT needed. **Caveats (do NOT over-read):** end-to-end DETECTION validated on ONE contested topic (obesity, 3 framings — the only retrieval-miss topic captured); prior generalizes 10/10 but other contested topics' live pools firing is untested end-to-end (needs live retrieval); the prior rests on gpt-4.1 world-knowledge (a knowledge dependency — H3 registry could backstop); reused pools, single run, n=9/n=6 modest; per-query firing is pool-composition-dependent (probabilistic, not deterministic); SHADOW DETECTION only (enforcement = A2/route-gated). → BACKLOG (broaden contested-topic detection on live retrieval before enabling). Flag stays OFF.

**LEVER 2 — FRONT-END QUESTION-NEUTRALIZATION — A/B SHADOW BUILT + VALIDATED → SAFE + EFFECTIVE-WHERE-REVERSAL-OCCURS, but NARROW (NOT a cross-topic pass) (2026-06-18, `question_neutralization_20260618_110411.json` + de-risk `_104531.json`; plan-back `question_neutralization_planback.md`; committed locally, NOT pushed, flag default OFF):** the OTHER failure mode (vs Lever 1) — GENERATION-reversal, source-in-hand: protective source retrieved but a LOADED framing makes the generator state the direction backwards. Lever rewrites the INTERNAL generator question to neutral (retrieval + user-facing question untouched). NOT a pure-observer shadow — it changes the answer, so the shadow is an **A/B harness** (both arms on the SAME frozen context, retrieve-once-freeze). Gate 1 HEALTHY. Built: `api/services/question_neutralization.py` (`is_loaded` detector + intent/qualifier-preserving `neutralize`), `server.py` flag-gated rewrite behind `QUESTION_NEUTRALIZATION_SHADOW` (default OFF, fail-open), `scripts/question_neutralization_eval.py`, corpus `tests/question_neutralization_corpus.json` (5 source-in-hand topics, vague-loaded framings + clean control n=8). Design = **detect-then-neutralize** (confirmed: detector 0/16 over-trigger on clean → safe to gate). **REAL NUMBERS — per topic (loaded→neutral REVERSED, /10): polypharmacy 4/10 → 0/10 (ELIMINATED ✅); beta_blockers_hfref 0/10→0/10; beta_carotene 0/10→0/10; cast 0/10→0/10; peanut retrieval-miss.** **HONEST VERDICT: reversal-reduction demonstrated on POLYPHARMACY ONLY** — the other 3 source-in-hand topics do NOT reverse under loaded framing (verified REAL faithful answers, not judge misses: BB "cornerstone… lower mortality HR 0.66"; beta-carotene "RCTs showed harm"). Per the founder's bar, only-polypharmacy ≠ cross-topic pass. The limiting factor is NOT the lever — **source-in-hand loaded-reversal is a NARROW phenomenon largely confined to the conflation-trap structure** (multimorbidity OR 1.82/2.04 co-located with polypharmacy OR 0.78 → vague-loaded framing grabs the harm stat); clean single-direction topics stay faithful. **OVER-NEUTRALIZATION (gating risk) PASSES strongly:** detector over-trigger 0/16, intent/qualifier-retention failures 0/16 (every clinical qualifier preserved), 0 REAL clean-regressions (both 1/8 "regressions" are generation-stochasticity artifacts — CLN-5's neutralized question was IDENTICAL to the original). Cost $1.11 + $0.45. **Net: the lever is SAFE + eliminates reversal where it occurs, but its addressable surface is narrow.** Two-lever coverage = Lever 1 (retrieval-miss, obesity-type, PASS) + Lever 2 (source-in-hand loaded reversal, polypharmacy-type, narrow). Caveats: 4 source-in-hand topics, 2 framings/topic, N=5, single frozen context/topic (reversal is pool-dependent). Flag stays OFF; A2/enforcement route-gated. → BACKLOG.

**DECISION STATE — OPEN (not decided):** founder wants BOTH B2C + B2B viable and agreed to "fix the most serious problems first" rather than pause B2C outright. The route (B2C-after-fix vs B2B-interim) is **PENDING**. **Update (2026-06-15): BOTH "reduce-bad-generation" paths are tried-and-insufficient for B2C** — the prompt-fix (PARTIAL; loaded-framing persists) AND the o4-mini model-swap (0/3 fidelity on B01-loaded). **Update (2026-06-16 #1): the defense roadmap must cover BOTH failure modes** (generation-reversal + retrieval-recall failure); ③ post-hoc checker is **blind** to the retrieval half. **Update (2026-06-16 #2): the retrieval-PROMPT-fix lever is now tried-and-failed (see above) → direction changes.** The retrieval-recall failure (obesity-type) is **hard to fix at the retrieval layer** (deeper levers = lay→MeSH term-expansion or two-sided retrieval = "Path B, deferred"), so it shifts to being **ABSORBED by ① structural-citation fallback** (detect missing protective coverage → low-confidence/withhold/flag, rather than guaranteeing retrieval). **NEW PRIMARY BUILD FOCUS = the GENERATION-reversal half** (polypharmacy-type, source-in-hand): ③ post-hoc direction checker + structural-citation binding (§706a (b)+(c)) — the confidently-actionable half. **Net roadmap: (C) generation-side defenses (③ / structural binding) + (A) ① fallback to absorb retrieval-miss; (B) deeper retrieval-stabilization deferred.** This **strengthens (does not decide) the lean toward "B2B-interim first; B2C gated on defensive mechanisms, not on model choice."** PREREQUISITE for any further eval run: OpenAI auto-recharge ON / balance sufficient (TECH_DEBT P0 ops).

**Framing discipline (keep in all docs):** the QA is EVIDENCE, not an endorsement; the unsupported/contradicts rate is a **lower bound** (abstract-only); the adversarial rate is an **upper bound** (loaded). NLI/contradiction-gate feasibility was assessed (NOT built) — verdict PARTIAL-not-reliable (see TECH_DEBT). OpenAI auto-recharge is OFF + prepaid credit expires → credit-zero returns `[ERROR]` to all users, a launch-gating stability risk (TECH_DEBT).

### 🔗 BLOCKING CHAIN — the long-lead-time human dependency

```
Generative UI LAYER 2  ←  §5.1 六國 moat  ←  b2 (JP/KR/TH Tier-1 data)  ←  ja / ko / th NATIVE CLINICAL REVIEW
                                                                              ↑ HUMAN dependency, longest lead time
```

- **b2's blocker is NOT code.** `resolveCountry` already returns JP/KR/TH and RULE 2 falls them to Tier-2, so **b2 is pure data work** — the block is **linguistic review of authority strings, which must not be machine-translated** (the rule that produced NPRA's confirmed Malay name rather than the model's first guess).
- **`th` sits at 0 cells reviewed** in `docs/native-review-checklist.md` Table B; **`ja` and `ko` are outside that checklist entirely** — they need **a review path that does not exist yet**, not merely a pass through the existing gate.
- **➡️ Because this is a human-recruitment dependency with the longest lead time in the queue, it can run in PARALLEL with everything above it — recruiting ja/ko/th clinical reviewers does not consume a build slot.** *(Noted for the founder's awareness; **not actioned here** — recruiting is a founder action, not a code task.)*
- **Also gated behind the same chain:** row 17 (在地差異 (f) L1 discoverability) *scales with* b2 but is not blocked by it.

**Three items need a founder decision rather than a build slot:** the **c2-ii scope call** (biologics/insulin/GLP-1 — widen the TFDA-mono backbone or accept the boundary), the **`/api/status` verification gap** (CODE-VERIFIED ONLY; closable in 30 seconds by replaying a devtools JWT — no code change), and the **Lever-1 enforce decision**, which now has a **third option**: surface the signal as a calibration cue instead of refusing (Layer 3).

## Blockers

**🟠 B2C public launch gated (2026-06-15, NOT a code blocker — a quality/legal gate; gate NOT cleared):** the medical advisor rejected direct B2C launch on the "reverses a counterintuitive finding + cites a real source" failure (measured ~0.6% random lower-bound / ~19% adversarial upper-bound, reproduced). **The prompt-fix was attempted (`96806b5`) and is INSUFFICIENT** — it resolves neutral-framed reversers (C01/C13) but the **loaded-framing direction reversal persists** (Stage-2 B01 still CONTRADICTS PMID 35268461; real B2C users ask loaded questions). Sharpened blocker: *loaded-framing direction reversal*. **UPDATE 2026-06-19 — this is NO LONGER framed as a near-term B2C-launch gate: route DECIDED = B2B-interim; B2C is a SaMD-gated terminal (B2B → real safety data + defense architecture → SaMD registration → then B2C). The reversal-defense was built + shipped shadow-to-prod (v181) but DEPLOYED ≠ USERS PROTECTED (no lever enforces; enforce/A2 deferred pending real B2B shadow traffic). So the gate is now the SaMD/route posture, not an open B2C decision.   > **① THE 422, CLASSIFIED FROM CODE THEN REPRODUCED (Phase 0, blocking).** The route takes a raw `Request` — NO Pydantic body model, so FastAPI's pre-handler-validation 422 path does not exist; the only 422s are two explicit sites, `server.py:2128` ("No customer email") and `:2150` ("Clerk user not found"), **both AFTER signature verification** (ordering checked per the car: unsigned garbage → 401, so no fix-worthy finding). **The sample hits `:2150` BY DESIGN:** its `customer.email` is the fake `test@acme.com`, no Clerk account exists, the live `api.clerk.com` lookup returns `[]` → deterministic 422 (both auto-retries identical; the ~1–3 s duration IS the Clerk round-trip). **Probe (11 scenarios, ALL matched):** exact sample signed → 422 `Clerk user not found` · same sample unsigned → 401 · real-shape fixtures for ALL FOUR handled events → 200 · per-field mutation matrix: `metadata: {}` no effect, `payment_method_id: null` no effect (handler reads neither), **`customer.email` resolving in Clerk is the ONLY flip field**; email absent hits the OTHER site (`:2128`), which the sample does not · real-shape + simulated Clerk outage → 422 (runtime-state). **THE MONEY ANSWER: no real-SHAPE cancelled/expired/failed payload can hit this** — checkout writes the Dodo customer email from the Clerk primary email server-side (`:1900-1918`). Two RUNTIME-STATE caveats (Clerk outage — transient, Dodo auto-retries 422s; subscriber removes the subscribe-time email — permanent silent keep-pro) are filed with a hardening shape as the new TECH_DEBT **[OTHER][P2] identity-join entry** (incl. that the handler IGNORES `data.metadata.clerk_user_id`, the robust key every real payload carries).

  > **① WHAT THE FLOOR WAS BEFORE: NOTHING.** `stats` counted PASS / WARN / FAIL / ERROR and **only `stats["PASS"]` ever reached a gate**, via `pass_rate`. WARN and FAIL were printed, rendered into HTML, and reached **no exit path at all**. The recorded **15 PASS / 2 WARN / 3 FAIL** run — three unadjudicated FAILs — computed 75.0%, cleared the 70 threshold, and **exited 0**. `838d0e6` built a pure `section_27_gate()`, a frozen `SECTION_27_CASE_IDS` **pinned against the dataset's own derivation by a guard test** (a pure derivation widens silently the day someone adds R21; a hand-typed list rots the other way — pinned against each other, divergence fails loudly and forces a ruling), and the constants 18 / 2 / 0.
  > **② WHY IT EXITS 2 AND NOT 1, which is the whole design.** `PASS 15 < 18` is *also* true, so a floor-breach reading was available and was rejected: the ratified floor requires every FAIL to be **"named and adjudicated individually"**, and **zero adjudication scaffolding exists in code** — the 133-case dataset carries no `exempt` / `known_fail` / `expected_status` field, and the R10/R20 oscillator handling lives only in ledger prose. An unadjudicated FAIL is **a result that needs a person**, not a scoreable breach. Same stance as the canary gate's insufficient-evidence leg.
  > **🔴 ③ `838d0e6` SHIPPED A DEFECT AND `1033f74` CORRECTED IT. Recorded as a defect found and fixed, NOT as one clean build — and the two commits are deliberately left UNSQUASHED so history shows the ruling.** As first specified, the gate's top rank fired whenever the executed set was not EXACTLY the §2.7 set. Consequence: a **full 133-case run and every `--smoke` run exited 2**, and **`pass_rate < 70` became unreachable on any non-R selection** — the documented `uv run python tests/run_golden_tests.py` invocation always exited 2. **Adding a gate had killed the only gate that previously worked**, which is strictly worse than the state it replaced. I recorded the consequence in-code rather than working around it; the founder then ruled **subset scoring**: a selection missing any of the 20 is **NOT SCORED, exits 0**, and defers to the 70% gate; only a run that executed all 20 can exit non-zero.
  > **④ THE RULING FORCED A SIGNATURE CHANGE, and that is the substantive half.** `stats` counts EVERY executed case, so subset-scoring off a global `stats` would have sent **three `COL_*` FAILs on a full run to §2.7 adjudication**. `section_27_gate(results) -> (code, reason, verdict)` now derives its own counts from records already restricted to the §2.7 ids — **counts and case set are structurally unable to disagree, because there is no argument to pass wrongly.** The verdict dict is returned, never written, and the JSON stores it verbatim so artifact and exit cannot diverge. Two keys that must not be confused, since leg A exits 0: **`scored`** and **`floor_met`** (True / False / **null**) — read `floor_met`, never `exit_code`.

The ":292" rate is therefore **20 of 27 positions, not 20 of 24 seats**. - **2026-08-18** [fly 236 · ✅ SHIPPED] **`/history` live re-run removal + Clerk-docstring OpenAPI fix DEPLOYED — prod stopped doing two things it should never have done** — pushed `9baf7d0..35ce47e` (5 commits separated prod from HEAD: `9baf7d0` docs · **`726bf5b` product** (`pages/history.tsx` −105) · `c32474e` docs · **`d3c0d95` product** (`api/server.py` docstring/comments, zero executable lines) · `35ce47e` docs — classification verified per commit before pushing, and `d3c0d95`'s server.py diff re-confirmed at `-U0`: 2 hunks, both inside the Clerk handler, no executable line, so the CRLF artifact did NOT reassert).
  > **WHAT PROD STOPPED DOING, plainly. - **2026-08-17** [Path A · ~~LOCAL — **committed, NOT pushed, NOT deployed**~~ ✅ **SHIPPED at fly 236** — `726bf5b` (`pages/history.tsx` −105) is an ancestor of `35ce47e`, the fly-236 readback revision. The `/history` re-run removal has been LIVE ON PROD since 2026-08-18] **`/history` no longer re-runs your query when you expand a Verify entry — the live `POST /api/verify` deleted from the history page** — `pages/history.tsx` −105 / +0, plus a new read-only probe.
  > **⚠️ WHAT THE USER LOSES — say it first, because it is a real regression and it is the point.** Expanding a Verify entry in History used to show a full **Interactions block**: per-pair severity, description, recommendation, colour-coded accents. **It no longer does.** The entry now shows what was actually stored — `item.answer`, the summary roll-up — and nothing more. **This is a visible downgrade of the history page, accepted deliberately**, because the block was not a record of anything: it was a *fresh query executed at view time*. **This is not a pure win and must not be reported as one.** The honest one-line version: *history stopped showing detail it never had.*
  > **THE MECHANISM.** `fetchVerifyDetails()` parsed the drug names back out of the stored question string with a regex (`/Drugs:\s*(.+)/`) and POSTed them to `/api/verify` to rebuild the interaction list, because **only the summary was ever persisted** — `ChatHistory` has no interactions column. Every expand therefore: **(a)** ran a **different query** than the one being viewed — `patient_context` dropped, `response_language` never sent, so the "details" could be clinically different from what the user originally saw and could come back in English; **(b)** rendered those interactions with **no Option-C attribution**, making the history surface *less* honest than `/verify` itself; **(c)** **deducted a credit**; **(d)** wrote a **fresh AuditLog + ChatHistory pair**, so reading history *wrote* history; **(e)** degraded to a silently empty Interactions block once quota ran out.
  > **🔴 THIS IS NOT A BAD DECISION BEING REVERSED.** `fetchVerifyDetails` has **exactly one commit — `631b6e5`, 2026-02-13, the initial commit** (verified: `git log -S` over the symbol returns one SHA). It was correct when written, against a product where those four systems did not yet exist. **It went silently wrong as credits, audit logging, response-language and Option-C attribution grew up around it** — and a `fetch` with `method: 'POST'` sitting inside a display helper reads like data loading, so it survived six months of review. **Same shape as the Rule 12 incident** (a rule true when written, false for two months, still being followed).
  > **🔴 THE STORED DATA IS CONTAMINATED AND CANNOT BE RETROSPECTIVELY CLEANED.** The **182 `chat_history` verify rows** include an **unknown fraction created by the re-run itself rather than by a user query**. There is no column distinguishing them — the re-run wrote through the same path as a real query. **Any per-user Verify count, any "queries run" metric, and any credit-usage reconstruction over that window is inflated by an unmeasurable amount.** This ships forward as a permanent data caveat; the fix stops new contamination and does nothing about the existing rows.
  > **THE GUARD (Rule 17 + Rule 20).** `tests/probes/history_readonly_smoke.mjs` — **12 checks, all passing**: no `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` in any quoting style, no mutating verb string at all, `fetchVerifyDetails`/`verifyDetails` gone by name, plus **positive controls** so the absence checks cannot pass vacuously on an emptied file (still ≥2 fetches, still reads `/api/history` + `/api/user/status`, still renders `item.answer`, and a control proving the comment-stripper still works). **Comments are stripped before matching** — this repo has been bitten **four times** by prose in a docstring satisfying a substring check, and the probe's own explanation contains the word POST. **Mutation-proved: 7 mutations, all caught.** M6 initially SURVIVED — `item.answer` occurs 5× and my mutation replaced one; **the mutation was insufficient, not the check** — fixed to replace all occurrences, then caught. **The probe carries an explicit self-deletion clause**: if history genuinely needs to write (per-entry delete is already scoped), whoever builds it must **delete this probe deliberately in the same commit with the reasoning written down**. Removing it *is* the decision point.
  > ⚠️ **SCOPE OF THE PROBE'S CLAIM — added 2026-08-17, because 12/12 green claims less than "the history page is read-only".** The probe reads **the source text of `pages/history.tsx` and nothing else** — not its imported components, and not the transitive mount tree. **`pages/history.tsx` renders `ShareButton` (`:274-281`, passing `answerText={item.answer}`), and clicking Share DOES issue a POST.** The semantics are fine — a user clicking Share is not a read-path write, which is the distinction the probe exists to protect — but the boundary must be stated rather than implied. 🔴 **And it is wider than one component.** Mounting this page transitively reaches **four** distinct POST endpoints: **`/api/share/create`** (`components/ShareModal.tsx:107` — **not** `ShareButton.tsx`, which contains **no fetch at all** and merely renders the modal at `:159`), **`/api/checkout/dodo`** (`components/UpgradeModal.tsx:32` — ~~reachable from this page's **own direct import**~~ ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-18: reachable at RENDER time ONLY via `ProFeatureOverlay.tsx:156` and `Navbar.tsx:362`.** This page imports `UpgradeModal` at `:7` but **never renders it** — zero `<UpgradeModal` in its JSX. The original wording confused an import with a mount, **and contradicted this very entry three lines down, which correctly records that import as dead**), **`/api/subscription/cancel`** (`components/Navbar.tsx:138`, via `PageShell`) and **`/api/bug-report`** (`components/BugReportButton.tsx:84`, via `PageShell`). **Three of the four arrive through `PageShell` / `ProFeatureOverlay`** — ~~chrome this page never names~~ ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-08-18: this page DOES name and render both `PageShell` (`:9`, `:305`) and `ProFeatureOverlay` (`:8`, `:142`). What it never names are the components that actually CONTAIN the fetches — `Navbar`, `BugReportButton`, `ShareModal`.** *(And 3-of-4 rather than 4-of-4 holds for a non-obvious reason worth recording: `/api/share/create` arrives via the `<ShareButton>` this page does name; it would ALSO arrive via `PageShell → Navbar.tsx:201`, except that slot is gated `{shareData && (`, `shareData` starts `null`, and this page never touches `ShareContext`.)* So the honest claim is *"no write ORIGINATES in this page's own source"*, **not** *"this page cannot write"*. **The probe was deliberately NOT widened and NO assertion was changed** — widening it to the mount tree would make it fail on ordinary chrome, and a probe that fails on ordinary chrome gets deleted within a month.
  > **RULE 16 — CONFIRMED, NOT ASSUMED. FOUNDER DECISION: add NO new user-visible string**, and none was added (**0 insertions**; no locale file touched). ✅ **The removal also eliminates two PRE-EXISTING Rule 16 violations** — the deleted block rendered **hardcoded English** (`Loading interaction details...`, `Interactions ({n})`) that had never had i18n keys. **NO i18n key is orphaned by this change** (the block used none), so **nothing was deleted from any locale** — correct, since 84 reviewer rows are in flight externally.
  > **RULE 7 — UNCHANGED.** No DB write path touched; the commit-site census stays at **25**. `api/server.py` verified **blob-identical to HEAD** (`f0241d04…`) — a `git status` " M" on it is a stat-cache artifact, not content. ⚠️ **SCOPE OF THAT NUMBER CORRECTED 2026-08-18: 25 is the `api/`-TREE total — `server.py` 16 · `services/usage_service.py` 7 · `services/cost_tracker.py` 2 — and this sentence attached it to `api/server.py` alone, which reads as 25 sites in one file.** Of the 16 in `server.py`, one is `_safe_db_write`'s own commit, so **15** are bypasses there and **24** across the tree. **Separately: `_safe_db_write` has 11 EXECUTABLE call sites, not the 15 a bare grep returns** — 3 hits are `#` comments and 1 is the definition, the same prose-inflation this repo has hit repeatedly.
  > **VERIFY, DON'T FIX — a second defect confirmed and deliberately LEFT IN PLACE.** In `POST /api/verify`, the `_safe_db_write()` that persists `answer=summary` (`api/server.py`, the verify handler's history write) runs **BEFORE** `summary` is mutated twice — spelling corrections, then TFDA grounding. **So `chat_history.answer` stores the un-corrected, un-grounded summary.** Blast radius, checked rather than assumed: **FeedbackBar does NOT read it** (`verify.tsx` passes `result.summary` from the live response); **ShareButton DOES**, but only via `pages/history.tsx` (`answerText={item.answer}`) — **so a share created from the history page carries the un-prefixed summary.** The fix is a **pure move** of the write to after the two mutations; nothing in between reads the DB. **NOT FIXED THIS BATON** (out of Path A's scope) and **no TECH_DEBT entry filed** — flagged here for the founder to place, so it is not lost in a shipped-log entry.
  > **FLAGS, not fixes:** the `UpgradeModal` import in `pages/history.tsx` is now unused — **verified PRE-EXISTING at HEAD** (it was already import-only before this change), so it is reported, not removed. Separately, this **reinforces open item #12's diagnosis**: History has nothing stored to show because the write path never persisted it; #12 remains a new feature (schema + write + render), and row 12 is left unedited.
